\doxysubsubsection*{:\+ A prefix string which is used in /\+proc/\+slabinfo to identify this}
kmem\+\_\+buckets\+\_\+create -\/ Create a set of caches that handle dynamic sized allocations via \doxylink{include_2linux_2slab_8h_a14043d12b33ee858117ecb472d4aecfe}{kmem\+\_\+buckets\+\_\+alloc()}

cache. The individual caches with have their sizes as the suffix. @flags:\+ SLAB flags (see \doxylink{include_2linux_2slab_8h_adf8cdc761f51d67846ff22c9b5798a58}{kmem\+\_\+cache\+\_\+create()} for details). @useroffset:\+ Starting offset within an allocation that may be copied to/\+from userspace. @usersize:\+ How many bytes, starting at @useroffset, may be copied to/\+from userspace. @ctor:\+ A constructor for the objects, run when new allocations are made.

Cannot be called within an interrupt, but can be interrupted.

Return:\+ a pointer to the cache on success, NULL on failure. When CONFIG\+\_\+\+SLAB\+\_\+\+BUCKETS is not enabled, ZERO\+\_\+\+SIZE\+\_\+\+PTR is returned, and subsequent calls to \doxylink{include_2linux_2slab_8h_a14043d12b33ee858117ecb472d4aecfe}{kmem\+\_\+buckets\+\_\+alloc()} will fall back to \doxylink{include_2linux_2slab_8h_ab39d1834eaedb0985332521b254f2e01}{kmalloc()}. (i.\+e. callers only need to check for NULL on failure.)

Function Description:\+ Creates a set of kmalloc buckets for custom allocators. It creates a \doxylink{include_2linux_2slab_8h_a9b4309b6cacadad8f0dc943ead258976}{kmem\+\_\+buckets} array with caches for each size, using the provided name prefix. Returns the buckets array or NULL on failure. \begin{DoxyCompactItemize}

\doxysubsubsection*{:\+ A string which is used in /\+proc/\+slabinfo to identify this cache.}
\+\_\+\+\_\+kmem\+\_\+cache\+\_\+create\+\_\+args -\/ Create a kmem cache.

@object\+\_\+size:\+ The size of objects to be created in this cache. @args:\+ Additional arguments for the cache creation (see \&struct \doxylink{structkmem__cache__args}{kmem\+\_\+cache\+\_\+args}). @flags:\+ See the descriptions of individual flags. The common ones are listed in the description below.

Not to be called directly, use the \doxylink{include_2linux_2slab_8h_adf8cdc761f51d67846ff22c9b5798a58}{kmem\+\_\+cache\+\_\+create()} wrapper with the same parameters.

Commonly used @flags:\+

\&SLAB\+\_\+\+ACCOUNT -\/ Account allocations to memcg.

\&SLAB\+\_\+\+HWCACHE\+\_\+\+ALIGN -\/ Align objects on cache line boundaries.

\&SLAB\+\_\+\+RECLAIM\+\_\+\+ACCOUNT -\/ Objects are reclaimable.

\&SLAB\+\_\+\+TYPESAFE\+\_\+\+BY\+\_\+\+RCU -\/ Slab page (not individual objects) freeing delayed by a grace period -\/ see the full description before using.

Context:\+ Cannot be called within a interrupt, but can be interrupted.

Return:\+ a pointer to the cache on success, NULL on failure.

Function Description:\+ Creates a \doxylink{structkmem__cache}{kmem\+\_\+cache} with advanced arguments. This is the main cache creation function called by \doxylink{include_2linux_2slab_8h_adf8cdc761f51d67846ff22c9b5798a58}{kmem\+\_\+cache\+\_\+create()}. It handles debugging flags, merging, and cache creation. Returns the cache or NULL on failure. \begin{DoxyCompactItemize}
\item

bool add\+\_\+ptr\+\_\+to\+\_\+bulk\+\_\+krc\+\_\+lock (\begin{DoxyParamCaption}\item[{struct \doxymbox{\hyperlink{structkfree__rcu__cpu}{kfree\+\_\+rcu\+\_\+cpu}} \texorpdfstring{$\ast$}{*}\texorpdfstring{$\ast$}{*}}]{krcp}{, }\item[{unsigned long \texorpdfstring{$\ast$}{*}}]{flags}{, }\item[{void \texorpdfstring{$\ast$}{*}}]{ptr}{, }\item[{bool}]{can\+\_\+alloc}{}\end{DoxyParamCaption})\hspace{0.3cm}{\ttfamily [inline]}, {\ttfamily [static]}}

Record ptr in a page managed by krcp, with the pre-\/krc\+\_\+this\+\_\+cpu\+\_\+lock() state specified by flags. If can\+\_\+alloc is true, the caller must be schedulable and not be holding any locks or mutexes that might be acquired by the memory allocator or anything that it might invoke. Returns true if ptr was successfully recorded, else the caller must use a fallback.

Function Description:\+ Adds a pointer to a bulk list in a \doxylink{structkfree__rcu__cpu}{kfree\+\_\+rcu\+\_\+cpu}. It acquires the lock, finds or creates a bulk block, and inserts the pointer. Returns true on success, false if it needs to use the fallback path.

Create the kmalloc array. Some of the regular kmalloc arrays may already have been created because they were needed to enable allocations for slab creation.

Function Description:\+ Creates all kmalloc caches for all sizes and types. It iterates through size classes and types, creating caches as needed. Called during boot to set up the kmalloc subsystem.

\doxysubsubsection{\texorpdfstring{kfree\_sensitive()}{kfree\_sensitive()}}
{\footnotesize\ttfamily \label{slab__common_8c_a2d2646034fc567fc383390023c1fc39c} 
void kfree\+\_\+sensitive (\begin{DoxyParamCaption}\item[{const void \texorpdfstring{$\ast$}{*}}]{p}{}\end{DoxyParamCaption})}

kfree\+\_\+sensitive -\/ Clear sensitive information in memory before freeing {\ttfamily }:\+ object to free memory of

The memory of the object {\ttfamily points} to is zeroed before freed. If {\ttfamily is} NULL, \doxylink{slab__common_8c_a2d2646034fc567fc383390023c1fc39c}{kfree\+\_\+sensitive()} does nothing.

Note:\+ this function zeroes the whole allocated buffer which can be a good deal bigger than the requested buffer size passed to \doxylink{include_2linux_2slab_8h_ab39d1834eaedb0985332521b254f2e01}{kmalloc()}. So be careful when using this function in performance sensitive code.

Function Description:\+ Frees memory containing sensitive data. It zeroes the allocated memory before freeing to prevent data leaks. Used for cryptographic or security-\/sensitive data.

\Hypertarget{slab__common_8c_a2b814faa932a75fad705b1b9d5e5d630}\index{slab\_common.c@{slab\_common.c}!kmalloc\_size\_roundup@{kmalloc\_size\_roundup}}
\index{kmalloc\_size\_roundup@{kmalloc\_size\_roundup}!slab\_common.c@{slab\_common.c}}
\doxysubsubsection{\texorpdfstring{kmalloc\_size\_roundup()}{kmalloc\_size\_roundup()}}
{\footnotesize\ttfamily \label{slab__common_8c_a2b814faa932a75fad705b1b9d5e5d630} 
size\+\_\+t kmalloc\+\_\+size\+\_\+roundup (\begin{DoxyParamCaption}\item[{size\+\_\+t}]{size}{}\end{DoxyParamCaption})}

Function Description:\+ Returns the actual allocation size for a given request. It rounds up to the next kmalloc bucket size or page size for large allocations. Used to determine how much memory will actually be allocated.

kmalloc\+\_\+size\+\_\+roundup -\/ Report allocation bucket size for the given size

@size:\+ Number of bytes to round up from.

This returns the number of bytes that would be available in a \doxylink{include_2linux_2slab_8h_ab39d1834eaedb0985332521b254f2e01}{kmalloc()} allocation of @size bytes. For example, a 126 byte request would be rounded up to the next sized kmalloc bucket, 128 bytes. (This is strictly for the general-\/purpose \doxylink{include_2linux_2slab_8h_ab39d1834eaedb0985332521b254f2e01}{kmalloc()}-\/based allocations, and is not for the pre-\/sized \doxylink{include_2linux_2slab_8h_a25a85450e1021b8c861f084b722b00d5}{kmem\+\_\+cache\+\_\+alloc()}-\/based allocations.)

Use this to \doxylink{include_2linux_2slab_8h_ab39d1834eaedb0985332521b254f2e01}{kmalloc()} the full bucket size ahead of time instead of using \doxylink{slub_8c_aad92a67e3451f0b7971c08ecad26b2e4}{ksize()} to query the size after an allocation.

Function Description:\+ Returns the actual allocation size for a given request size. This rounds up to the next kmalloc bucket size. Used to determine how much memory will actually be allocated.

void kmem\+\_\+cache\+\_\+destroy (\begin{DoxyParamCaption}\item[{struct \doxymbox{\hyperlink{structkmem__cache}{kmem\+\_\+cache}} \texorpdfstring{$\ast$}{*}}]{s}{}\end{DoxyParamCaption})}

Function Description:\+ Destroys a \doxylink{structkmem__cache}{kmem\+\_\+cache}. It waits for pending RCU frees, decrements the refcount, shuts down the cache, removes it from the list, and releases resources. Returns when the cache is fully destroyed.

\doxysubsubsection{\texorpdfstring{kvfree\_call\_rcu()}{kvfree\_call\_rcu()}}
{\footnotesize\ttfamily \label{slab__common_8c_a2268455e48709f6d6c4a942ca4023507} 
void kvfree\+\_\+call\+\_\+rcu (\begin{DoxyParamCaption}\item[{struct rcu\+\_\+head \texorpdfstring{$\ast$}{*}}]{head}{, }\item[{void \texorpdfstring{$\ast$}{*}}]{ptr}{}\end{DoxyParamCaption})}

Queue a request for lazy invocation of the appropriate free routine after a grace period. Please note that three paths are maintained, two for the common case using arrays of pointers and a third one that is used only when the main paths cannot be used, for example, due to memory pressure.

Each \doxylink{slab__common_8c_a2268455e48709f6d6c4a942ca4023507}{kvfree\+\_\+call\+\_\+rcu()} request is added to a batch. The batch will be drained every KFREE\+\_\+\+DRAIN\+\_\+\+JIFFIES number of jiffies. All the objects in the batch will be free\textquotesingle{}d in workqueue context. This allows us to:\+ batch requests together to reduce the number of grace periods during heavy kfree\+\_\+rcu()/\+kvfree\+\_\+rcu() load.

Function Description:\+ Queues a memory pointer for deferred freeing after an RCU grace period. It attempts to batch the request into a bulk list for efficient processing. If bulk insertion fails (e.\+g., due to memory pressure), it falls back to a linked list of rcu\+\_\+head structures. For head-\/less (single-\/argument) calls, it may synchronize immediately if batching is not possible. The function first checks if the object can be freed via the sheaf mechanism (for SLUB caches), then tries to add the pointer to a bulk list using \doxylink{slab__common_8c_a537618004a1af72b4f771f3d49009f47}{add\+\_\+ptr\+\_\+to\+\_\+bulk\+\_\+krc\+\_\+lock()}. On success, it marks the object as ignored by kmemleak (since freeing is deferred) and schedules a monitor work to drain the batch after KFREE\+\_\+\+DRAIN\+\_\+\+JIFFIES. If bulk insertion fails and no rcu\+\_\+head is provided, it falls back to inline synchronize\+\_\+rcu() followed by \doxylink{include_2linux_2slab_8h_ac88c4c777e1cffcd536d365ce527ba94}{kvfree()}. This function is the core of the kvfree\+\_\+rcu() API, enabling efficient batching of RCU-\/protected frees.

\doxysubsubsection{\texorpdfstring{kvfree\_rcu\_barrier()}{kvfree\_rcu\_barrier()}}
{\footnotesize\ttfamily \label{slab__common_8c_adde0643cdb4ceba37c4491ed394a9b9c} 
void kvfree\+\_\+rcu\+\_\+barrier (\begin{DoxyParamCaption}\item[{void}]{}{}\end{DoxyParamCaption})}

kvfree\+\_\+rcu\+\_\+barrier -\/ Wait until all in-\/flight kvfree\+\_\+rcu() complete.

Note that a single argument of kvfree\+\_\+rcu() call has a slow path that triggers synchronize\+\_\+rcu() following by freeing a pointer. It is done before the return from the function. Therefore for any single-\/argument call that will result in a \doxylink{include_2linux_2slab_8h_a5273884a01705283d2b9e4262a8e6da3}{kfree()} to a cache that is to be destroyed during module exit, it is developer\textquotesingle{}s responsibility to ensure that all such calls have returned before the call to \doxylink{slab__common_8c_a0ed417eb6258e26997a82b132776f54a}{kmem\+\_\+cache\+\_\+destroy()}.

Function Description:\+ Waits for all in-\/flight kvfree\+\_\+rcu() calls to complete. It flushes sheaves and calls \doxylink{slab__common_8c_a014e20f10be6906ad5091a5b254444d3}{\+\_\+\+\_\+kvfree\+\_\+rcu\+\_\+barrier()}. Used during module unload and cache destruction. 

Definition at line \doxymbox{\hyperlink{slab__common_8c_source_l02342}{2342}} of file \doxymbox{\hyperlink{slab__common_8c_source}{slab\+\_\+common.\+c}}.

Function Description:\+ Frees a batch of objects that have passed their RCU grace period. It checks if the grace period has passed, unqueues RCU heads, and frees objects using either \doxylink{include_2linux_2slab_8h_ae88c30515cdcad45627e8ffd37f8d80d}{kfree\+\_\+bulk()} (for slab objects) or \doxylink{vmalloc_8h_a2aeead9450a5b920c65bea908edead23}{vfree()} (for vmalloc objects). The bulk node is then either cached or freed. Used to process bulk kvfree\+\_\+rcu() requests. 

Definition at line \doxymbox{\hyperlink{slab__common_8c_source_l01675}{1675}} of file \doxymbox{\hyperlink{slab__common_8c_source}{slab\+\_\+common.\+c}}.

Function Description:\+ Frees a linked list of objects that have passed their RCU grace period. It iterates through the list, unqueues RCU heads, and calls \doxylink{include_2linux_2slab_8h_ac88c4c777e1cffcd536d365ce527ba94}{kvfree()} on each object. Used as a fallback path when bulk freeing is not possible.

void \+\_\+\+\_\+init setup\+\_\+kmalloc\+\_\+cache\+\_\+index\+\_\+table (\begin{DoxyParamCaption}\item[{void}]{}{}\end{DoxyParamCaption})}

Patch up the size\+\_\+index table if we have strange large alignment requirements for the kmalloc array. This is only the case for MIPS it seems. The standard arches will not generate any code here.

Largest permitted alignment is 256 bytes due to the way we handle the index determination for the smaller caches.

Make sure that nothing crazy happens if someone starts tinkering around with ARCH\+\_\+\+KMALLOC\+\_\+\+MINALIGN

Function Description:\+ Sets up the kmalloc size index table for small allocations. It adjusts the index table based on architecture-\/specific minimum alignment. Called during boot.

Conversion table for small slabs sizes /\+ 8 to the index in the kmalloc array. This is necessary for slabs \texorpdfstring{$<$}{<} 192 since we have non power of two cache sizes there. The size of larger slabs can be determined using fls.

Variable Description:\+ A lookup table for small kmalloc sizes. Maps size/\+8 to cache index. Used for fast size-\/to-\/cache lookup for sizes up to 192 bytes.

Variable Description:\+ This structure defines the file operations for the /\+proc/\+slabinfo proc file. It specifies that the entry is permanent (PROC\+\_\+\+ENTRY\+\_\+\+PERMANENT), and provides handlers for opening (slabinfo\+\_\+open), reading (seq\+\_\+read), seeking (seq\+\_\+lseek), and releasing (seq\+\_\+release) the file. These operations allow users to read slab cache statistics from /\+proc/\+slabinfo, which displays information about active objects, total objects, object sizes, and slab counts for each cache in the system. 

Definition at line \doxymbox{\hyperlink{slab__common_8c_source_l01344}{1344}} of file \doxymbox{\hyperlink{slab__common_8c_source}{slab\+\_\+common.\+c}}.
